# INTRODUCTION

 

 

NFTs are taking the gaming and art and worlds by storm \- but what are they exactly?

You may have come across the term NFT in the headlines lately, particularly regarding some huge amounts of money (69.3 million dollars ring any bells)? However, you probably wondered what the fuss was all about. If you are unsure what an NFT is, or how exactly they work, we are here to help.

This guide discusses everything you need to know about NFTs, including how they operate, why they caused controversy, and how you can get involved. If you decide you would like to create yours, you can deploy this brilliant digital art software.

 

What is NFT?

An NFT is essentially a digital collectible asset that holds value as a form of cryptocurrency. Just as art is seen as an investment that holds value, so are NFTs. But how?

Let's first split the term. NFT stands for Non-Fungible Token

– a digital token, similar to bitcoin or ethereum, a cryptocurrency. However, unlike a regular Bitcoin coin, an NFT is special and cannot be exchanged like-for-like (hence, non-fungible).

 

So what makes a non-fungible token more special than a run-of-the-mill crypto coin? The file stores additional information that raises it above the pure currency and takes it into the realm. The types of NFTs are super varied, but they can take the form of a digital piece of music and art file

– anything unique that could be digitally stored and be thought of to hold value. They're essentially like any

other physical collector's item, except you get a JPG file, for example, instead of receiving an oil painting on canvas to hang on your wall.

 

 

How do NFTs work?

NFTs belong to the Ethereum blockchain, so they are individual tokens with additional information stored in them. That additional information is the key element that enables art, music, video (and so on) to take shape as a JPG, MP3, video, GIF, and more. Because they have value, they can be purchased and sold much like other types of art – and the value is set by the market and demand, just like physical art.

 

 

However, there isn't just one digital version of an NFT art on the market. The same way as art prints of an original is made, bought, used, and sold, copies of an NFT are still valid parts of the Blockchain – but they don't have the same value as the original.

And do not go thinking you have hacked the system by right-clicking and saving the image of an NFT, either. That will not make you a millionaire because the information you downloaded will not contain Ethereum blockchain information.

 

 

Where can I purchase NFT tokens?

NFTs can be purchased on various platforms, which depends upon what you choose to buy (for instance, if you want to purchase baseball cards, you are the best heading to a site like digitaltradingcards; however, more generalized pieces are sold on other marketplaces). You will need a particular wallet for the platform you are buying, and you will need to fill the wallet with cryptocurrencies. As the sale

of Beeple's Everyday – the first 5000 days at Christie's (above) proved, these pieces are also beginning to reach more mainstream auction houses. That Beeple piece was the one that went for $69.3 million.

Due to the high demand for many types of NFT, they are frequently released as "drops" (like in events when batches of tickets are released at different times). This means that you will have to register when the drop begins and have your wallet topped up beforehand.

NFTs also make waves as in-game purchases across different video games (much to the, erm, delight of parents everywhere). The assets can be purchased and sold by players, and include playable assets such as unique

swords, avatars, or skins.

 

Who's been using NFTs?

NFTs are having a moment, with creators of NFT art including artists, gamers, and brands across the spectrum of culture. Indeed, it seems every day brings a fresh player to the NFT marketplace.

For artists, entering into the NFT space adds another format and space to create and share art and, gives their admirers a different means of supporting their work. With pieces ranging from small, quick-to-make GIFs, to more ambitious works, artists can offer the public a wide variety of ways to purchase art and make money in the process.

We have talked a little about those making NFTs in video games, something that is shaking up the idea of the purchase of assets in video games. Until now, any digital asset purchased from a game still belonged to the game

company – with players only purchasing them

temporarily to use when playing the game. But NFTs mean that asset ownership is transferred to the actual buyer, to be purchased and sold throughout the gaming platform, and have extra value depending on who owns them throughout the game. In reality, games based completely on NFTs are beginning to be created, proving how they are shaking the industry.

It is expected that well-known artists would earn the big bucks in return for their work, which was relied on when a newly unconventional group of "art lovers" burnt an original Banksy to make it an NFT. But other sales are more shocking. For instance, Beeple's first foray into the NFT market, while he is well-known as a digital artist, it was unlikely that this auction would bring the third-highest price ever paid for a living artist’s work.

NFTs are an appealing source of revenue for brands, as shown by the brands hopping on the bandwagon of late. In just 30 minutes, Taco Bell sold taco-themed GIFs and pictures, and the haul of 25 sold out in one market. Each NFT has a $500 gift card that the original owner can spend, which may initially explain their popularity. But these TacoCards are now available on the secondary market, with the most expensive card selling for $3,500, and that does not include the gift card.

The NBA has NBA Top Shot \- a way to market digital items in trading cards, embedded with iconic moments from the game. The NBA aims to find ways to extend this revenue stream as much as it can with a campaign to incorporate virtual jewelry, clothing, and accessories that can be used through social networks.

Even Tweets carry value, with Twitter co-founder Jack Dorsey sold the first-ever tweet for $2,915,835,47.

Musicians are also selling originals, the rights to their work, and clips of their music to short videos, and you can even purchase digital real estate and 3D assets like furniture.

A "digital home" has been recently sold for a world-class

$500,000. Digital art marketplace SuperRare called "The first digital house in the world," 'Mars House,' created by Toronto artist Krista Kim. Created with the help of video game software and an architect, the owner will explore the mansion on Mars utilizing virtual reality, including sunbathing outside the house (in the Mars atmosphere).

 

 

Why are NFTs controversial?

There is a lot of money to be made in the NFT industry, but you may also have heard controversy regarding NFTs, particularly their climate effects.

NFTs use a huge amount of energy in their creation. Protesters are concerned about the very real environmental effect the craze could have. CryptoArt.wtf, a platform created to calculate the carbon footprint of NFTs, indicated that one piece called 'Coronavirus' had consumed 192 kWh since it was created. This corresponds to the total energy intake of one EU resident for two weeks. But this must be an enormous piece, you ask? No, a 'simple' GIF may equate to the same consumption.

In an interview, @beeple says, "I can guarantee you that all my drops will not just be carbon neutral, but carbon NEGATIVE" he said. I'm sure many people are going to hold him to that.

Artists can work to create carbon-neutral artwork (Beeple has already promised to do this going forward). However, the issue goes deeper because of how cryptocurrency systems are constructed.

Bitcoin, Ethereum, and the like are built based on a 'proof-of- work mechanism (such as a complicated series of puzzles) to protect the financial records of their users. Ethereum uses approximately the same quantity of energy as the whole country of Libya.

ArtStation was so concerned about the climate effect regarding its recent decision to sell NFTs after a major backlash. However, it might not always be so controversial as organizations are working to change things.

 

 

Can anyone make an NFT?

You have got this far, so you might be wondering: could someone just get involved? Well, one would assume so, given that Trevor Andrew drew a Gucci Ghost and managed to sell it for a whopping $3,600.

Technically, anyone can sell an NFT. Anybody can create work, convert it into an NFT on the Blockchain, and sell it on a chosen market. You can add a commission to the file, which will pay you anytime someone purchases the piece, and any time anyone buys this item – even resales – they will pay you. Just like when you buy NFTs, you need a wallet, which must be full of cryptocurrency, and the requirement for money upfront is where the complexities lie.

The hidden fees may be prohibitively informative, whereby a 'gas' fee is charged for each sale (the amount of energy it needs to complete the transaction), along with a sales and purchase fee. Conversion fees and price changes depending on the time of day should also be taken into account. All this means that the fees will always add up to much more than the price you get for selling the NFT. However, different sites have different fees attached, and some are better than others.

Whether or not NFTs are here to stay, they are a new toy for the Uber-rich, and there is real money to be made. NFTs offer digital art a new meaning, and the prices seen at sales show that it is a part of the future of art and collectibles in general.

# CHAPTER 1:

# WHY 2021 AND 2022 WILL BE THE YEAR OF NON-FUNGIBLE TOKENS (NFTS)

 

 

 

ICOs 2017, Smart Contracts 2018, Stablecoins 2019, and Defi 2020. It's Time to Pass On the Torch: NFTs in 2021.

It's no secret that people like to gather things. The kinds of things you collect depends on your interests and passions, or whether those collectibles resonate emotionally with you.

Think of how you might have collected stamps, Pokémon cards, video games, posters, books, maybe even marbles as a child. Your collections may look pretty different as an adult. Maybe now you collect fashionable shoes and bags, tech gadgets, antiques, or artwork. Or maybe you still collect the same stuff you did as a kid.

But what if the digital and virtual world could extend your physical collection? And what if UFC fans can collect and own their favorite Conor McGregor moments? How about Nike fans traversing virtual gaming worlds while donning your favorite kicks? What if you can own the pet dragon you have always secretly wanted? Imagine getting a virtual digital artwork gallery that can be fitted into your pocket and taken anywhere with you to show your family and friends. How about owning the memorabilia of your favorite celebrity and behind-the-scenes rarities? Well, you probably don't have to imagine for much longer because, with the emergence of non-fungible tokens(NFTs), your wildest dreams and fantasies could become digitally real in the crypto world. Here is a heads-up to all the fans of Star Trek who will soon own the tokenized memory of William Shatner in the form of trading cards, one of which will be a poignant picture of Shatner hugging longtime friend Leonard Nimoy.

Over the past few months, NFTs have become the world's source of whispers, and are a strong contender for the spotlight in the crypto world in 2021. Waves of trends have characterized the relatively short history of the crypto world: the 2017 ICO boom, 2017–18 smart contract platform era, 2018-19 stablecoin era, and DeFi craze n 2020.

DeFi craze n 2020. Ilya Abugov, DappRadar project manager, recently told Cointelegraph, "NFTs now have more hype. To some extent, it is an extension of the DeFi thrill ."

 

 

What Makes a Non-Fungible Token?

NFTs are tokenized assets such as artwork, collectibles, or real estate. Non-fungible assets

possess individual characteristics and properties that make them valuable and unique.

Looking at the emergence of bitcoin, the crypto world has been dominated by fungible currencies that have dominated the environment in the last decade. "Fungibility" is a complicated idea with a simple premise. It speaks of the interchangeability of an asset, where two items are fungible if exchanging them is meaningless. For instance, there is no difference in value or meaning if two people exchange dollar notes with each other. The exchange would only become non-fungible if one cared about the dollar bill's serial number and where it was created.

In other words, NFTs are everything in the world that is rare and unique. Do you remember the example of your favorite Conor McGregor moment? There will probably never be an exact replication or repetition of the moment, which makes it valuable and unique. Fans can own or trade their favorite moment by tokenizing that spectacular knockout, and writing its unique properties into a smart contract. NFTs can be used for a range of assets, including virtual collectibles,

in-game items, digital artwork, real estate, and more. But because each token is unique, they can't be exchanged with the same ease, or interchangeability cannot be exchanged as fungible assets. For example, a very rare, old, and expensive car cannot be exchanged for a common car. Decrypt explains that NFTs:

Another reason why NFTs are distinct from fungible cryptocurrencies is that they are indivisible, and you cannot trade a fraction of them like Bitcoin. For instance, you cannot cut out a fraction of a concert ticket and give it to a friend.

None of you are going to get past security with fractions of a ticket.

 

![](Images/image00709.jpeg)Why Are NFTs Getting Attention Now?

 

 

Net NFT market cap evolution 2017–2020. (Source: Cointelegraph)

 

NFTs are not a fresh concept, and the first NFT-like token was the introduction of Bitcoin 2.X (aka colored coins) in 2012\. The NFT sector grew by 17 percent in 2019, and was expected to rise by 50 percent by the end of 2020 with respect to market capitalization. This is a fascinating because it reflects a gross value of over $200 million in just under two years. NFT data resource NonFungible.com reports that since September 2020, NFT sales activities have risen significantly, and remained higher than the previous months. NFT sales in the first week of September 2020 hit close to $1 million as Sorare (the blockchain-based fantasy soccer card game) hit over $221,000. This momentum indicates no sign

of slowing down as two million dollars worth of NFTs were traded in the first week of December 2020. The market capitalization of NFTs has surpassed its 2020 forecast and crossed over 550 million dollars, according to CoinGecko.

![](Images/image00710.jpeg)

 

An overview of total USD transferred via the NFT project history between December 8, 2019, and December 8, 2020. Visualizes the recent increase of NFTs transferred in US Dollars, especially when comparing the first three and last three months of the year. (Source: NonFungible.com)

The CryptoKitties Boom in November 2017 was one of the first NFT ventures to erupt and attract much publicity.

CryptoKitties is a game developed on the Ethereum blockchain that allows users to collect, breed, and exchange virtual cats. The game was considered to slow down Ethereum's network in 2017 because its high volume of activity exemplified and illustrated a blockchain case for entertainment and leisure instead of currency. These virtual cats seem to attract millions of dollars, and the rarer cats are valued at hundreds of dollars each.

![](Images/image00711.jpeg)

 

Dragon is the most expensive CryptoKitty to date, selling for 600 ETH (roughly $170,000 at the time).

Dapper Labs, the company behind CryptoKitties, set out with the simple aim of bringing Blockchain to a billion people. According to co-founder Mik Naayem, they chose entertainment and gaming as a target market because it is an "easier way to expose folks to decentralization" and "gamers are the ideal target market because they already understand virtual currencies and virtual worlds." "What we were trying to do when we built CryptoKitties was to see whether we could get people who had never used a blockchain before to use it... and understand why it's different and valuable," he continues.

They have been a huge success. CryptoKitties taught new users about Blockchain and introduced the NFTs concept to hundreds of thousands of people.

Following the CryptoKitties boom at the end of 2017, the NFT market stayed relatively stagnant in 2018 and 2019.

According to NonFungible's annual report, 2019 was a year of stability for the NFT ecosystem, as it entered a "period of restructuring and stabilization" following the ICO frenzy.

![](Images/image00712.jpeg)According to the report, most NFT ventures had a low retention rate in the previous two years, with the vast majority being used for three months or less in 2019. It goes on to say that only about 10% of newly generated NFT assets circulated during the year, a rate similar to 2018.

 

The dollar value of NFTs transferred trebled in 2018, peaking at $159 million, before dropping to $152 million in 2019.

Despite a decline following the initial 2017 excitement, new developments in the NFT space continued to emerge, with many activities and initiatives gaining momentum in recent months. Projects like the recent Bitcoin-code-inspired artwork sold for over $130,000, and the blockchain-based trading card of Paris Saint-Germain forward Kylian Mbappé that sold for $66,850 have set new sales records. While the CryptoKitty Dragon remains the most expensive non- fungible token ever sold, the one-of-a-kind digital sports car

F1 Delta Time Apex Race Car "1–1–1" (415.9 ETH, $113,124) and a plot of land called The Secret of Satoshi's Tea Garden in the virtual reality network Decentraland (1.3M MANA,

$80,663) are two of the most expensive NFTs sold in 2019. Celebrities had started using NFTs, such as Paris Hilton auctioning off a cryptograph drawing of her pet cat Munchkin for 40 ETH (worth $17,000 at the time) to raise money for charity in August.

With all of the buzz surrounding NFTs, large corporations and well-known brands have shown interest in participating in the industry. Several of the world's most well-known brands are now working on blockchain-based collectibles, games, and virtual worlds. These include the National Football League (NFL), the Ultimate Fighting Championship (UFC), the National Basketball Association (NBA), Formula 1, Louis Vuitton, Nike, and Samsung, to name a few. The involvement of these big brands is a significant step toward mainstream acceptance of NFTs. New users are introduced to the concept of NFTs through platforms that they are passionate about and already acquainted with.

 

 

How Do NFTs Work? Let's Get a Bit Geeky

An NFT's unique information is stored in its smart contract and registered on the token's Blockchain. NFTs are ERC-721 tokens, which use the Ethereum blockchain and protocols. The main feature of ERC-721 tokens is that there is only one of them when one is generated. Users can make their ERC- 721 tokens by writing a code in a smart contract that follows a simple template, then adding special details about the token. These special details can include the name of the owner, secure file links, or rich metadata.

Witek Radomski, co-founder, and CTO of Enjin created ERC-1155. The ERC-1155 standard is a new standard that allows a single, smart contract to control an infinite number of tokens. Unlike the ERC-721 token standard, which only produces NFTs and requires developers to deploy a new smart contract for each new token, the ERC-1155 token standard allows developers to deploy a single, smart

contract and mint new tokens (fungible and non-fungible) in seconds, indefinitely. ERC-1155 contracts now power over forty games, since a contract created for a game can include a wide range of items, from armor and weapons to health potions and superpowers. The ERC-1155, on the other hand, is not limited to gaming and can be applied in a variety of industries.

![](Images/image00713.jpeg)

 

The ERC721-standard versus ERC-1155.

Other blockchains that supported NFTs include the TRON Foundation, NEO LastSamurai, Dapper Labs' Flow blockchain, and eosio. NFTs are bought and sold on open marketplaces that link buyers and sellers, such as Decentraland's LAND marketplace or OpenSea, unlike Bitcoin and other fungible cryptocurrencies they can be exchanged on regular cryptocurrency exchanges.

Characteristics of NFTs — What Makes Them So Appealing?

The potential to prove authenticity, provide ownership, and be transferable are three desirable characteristics of NFTs.

Authenticity

NFTs are uniquely recognizable and authenticated due to their standardization and the comprehensive attributes of their smart contracts. Users are aware that there is only one of each NFT in existence, so they cannot be replicated or duplicated, and there is no risk of receiving a forged item. Because blockchain technology is transparent, it is also possible to verify and prove authenticity.

Counterfeiting and fraud are major issues in many sectors, including art and luxury items, but according to Cointelegraph, "the blockchains running NFTs clamp down on counterfeiting — and offer consumers hope that they'll get what they pay for." According to Coindesk, LVMH, the parent company of the famous Louis Vuitton label, is riding the NFT wave and is planning to launch a blockchain to prove the authenticity of their luxury products. The goal is for products to be given NFTs so that their authenticity can be tested. It would also be possible to track an individual handbag's lifecycle journey from alligator farm to the store where it was sold, as well as the various owners who bought and sold it previously.

Ownership

People will finally start owning what is rightfully theirs thanks to blockchain technology. It's worth mentioning that, due to their smart contracts and associated rights, NFTs can only be transacted and exchanged by the owner(s) of the asset. This means that even the NFT's issuer cannot duplicate or move the NFT without its owner's permission.

Gamers have been trading virtual currencies and assets for years, from gold in World of Warcraft, to cosmetic character skins in Fortnite. However, in a traditional game, you don't truly own anything in the virtual world because the economy is based solely on a closed market. You could have paid $60 for the program's license, played it for a month, completed the game, and then put it away with nothing to show for it. In other words, since you don't truly own an in- game currency or item, you can't trade it for flat currency, or take it with you to another game. However, in the world of blockchain games, you own all of the tokenized in-game assets (in the form of NFTs) in your wallet and can trade, fight, battle, or race them through multiple games. Whether it's an enchanted sword, currency, or skins, you own and control everything. During the Futuristic Conference 2020 panel on The Future of Gaming and Non-Fungible Tokens, Dr. Jesse Reich (CEO Splinterlands) elaborated about how owning your properties is a one-way valve: "As soon as you start owning your sword and armor, as well as your characters and something unique about them, the thought of spending hundreds or thousands of dollars on another game and not owning either of those items would become unappealing." Naayem of Dapper Lab agrees, stating that once players have experienced uniqueness, ownership, and scarcity in a virtual space, handing over cash in regular games completely loses its appeal.

Transferrable

Since NFTs are decentralized, they don't need a central issuing agency and are easier to transport. It enables users to engage in permission-less peer-to-peer trading,

interaction, and specialist marketplaces without dealing with intrusive third parties who could slow or complicate the method.

Why Are NFTs So Exciting?

NFTs are opening up new revenue streams for all creators (visual artists, game designers, musicians, and so on). The variety of assets they can represent, including virtual collectibles, event tickets, real estate, identity documents, game items, digital artwork, certifications, and more, is part of what makes them so appealing. William E. Quigley, a co- founder of WAX, made the bold statement:

"As a distinct entertainment medium, NFTs will coexist with movies, music, and video games. Every non-edible consumer product can become an NFT." — William E. Quiqley (WAX)

NFTs certainly open a wide range of virtual assets and real- world possibilities thanks to easy proof of ownership and transfer.

Bitcoin and cryptocurrency trading and mining are at the core of the current crypto market. However, it needs to move away from this era to promote widespread adoption. NFTs are fueling this because they can capture the minds and hearts and onboard them into the blockchain world in a way that fungible currencies haven't been able to. In the case of CryptoKitties, Dapper Labs' Naayem revealed that "a little over 40% of our audience had never owned a cryptocurrency before." By introducing new users to the crypto world through channels of recreation and entertainment, it is easier for beginners to access and understand the value that decentralization and blockchain technology can provide. Dapp Radar project manager Ilya Abugov told Cointelegraph:

"The easiest use cases for retail users to understand are art and collectibles, so that may be where the hype focuses for a while. If we see a fun game and more artists join the ecosystem, the trend can gain momentum in the mainstream. However, NFTs opens up a slew of new possibilities,

ranging from asset tokenization to documentation." — Ilya Abugov (Dapp Radar)

Current NFT Use Cases

Current NFT use cases are focused on the main categories of art, gaming, collectibles, and virtual worlds. However, other categories, such as fashion, sport, and real-world

assets, are steadily growing. Virtual land investor @Dclblogger lists about twenty-five industries that the NFT ecosystem is disrupting.

 

 

Collectibles

Collectibles are presently one of the most popular applications of NFTs in terms of sales volume, accounting for around 23.6 percent of all sales in the previous month. CryptoPunks, which debuted in June 2017 and has since sold for thousands of dollars, was one of the first collectible NFTs on Ethereum. They were created before ERC-721 was introduced, and a wrapper had to be created for them to be traded on exchanges like OpenSea. Since its launch in November 2017, CryptoKitties has become one of the most well-known collectibles, with sales totaling more than $38 million.

This category continues to develop and evolve as NFT technology builds tokenized versions of celebrities and star athletes for fans to collect. So rare is a fantasy soccer game in which players can collect "limited edition digital collectibles" of their favorite players from 100 different football clubs.

During the NFT NYC in February 2020, members of the NFL and NBA shared interest in collaborating with NFT technology. Since then, CryptoKitties developer Dapper Labs have introduced token-powered NBA TopShot in partnership with the NBA. Caty Tedman, Dapper Labs' head of partnerships and marketing, told Cointelegraph that "the product is intended to give fans a piece of control over the action that happens on court." NFT collectibles have shown their ability and power to introduce these communities to Blockchain by tapping into enthusiastic fan bases. NFTs also drag more conventional collector items into blockchain technology, such as coins, stamps, trading cards (e.g., crypto stamps).

Terra Virtua, a digital collectibles ecosystem, is at the forefront of the NFT collectibles trend. By bringing digital collectibles into a multiplatform VR and AR world, the platform aims to provide users with a "deep sensory experience."

Terra Virtua has released 'vFlects,' a collection of character- based 3D animated creatures, as well as licensed 2D and 3D collectibles from Top Gun, Sunset Boulevard, The Godfather, and Lost in Space. They don't only have a marketplace for an immersive collection of exclusive and licensed digital collectibles. Still, they've also created a cross-platform journey with their art gallery app and Terra Virtua Fancave. Owners of art galleries can use the app to store, view, communicate about, and display their collection of

unique digital artworks. Users can display their collectibles in the Terra Virtua Fancave, a customizable virtual reality environment. "The main goal of Terra Virtua is to bring NFTs to the mass market," says Gary Bracey, CEO of Terra Virtua. The core team's videogame experience gives us a strong focus on UI and UX, and we hope to bring the world of interactive Digital Collectibles to the mainstream by making the process both compelling and frictionless." The cross-platform capabilities and sensory element that Terra Virtua provides its users pushes NFTs beyond their current label as products into truly engaging and interactive experiences.

 

Gaming

Gamers are an ideal target market for NFTs since they are already acquainted with virtual worlds and currencies. The gaming industry is booming thanks to NFTs, which enable in-game objects to be tokenized and easily transferred or traded using peer-to-peer trading and marketplaces.

Traditional games forbid the sales or transfer of in-game items such as rare weapons and skins, because players have complete control of their digital assets, NFTs make the gaming experience more tangible and rewarding. They're also creating a new economy, as players can now benefit from their in-game assets by building and developing them.

"The idea behind blockchain's potential here is that player loyalty will increase as they have more skin in the game: as their digital assets can be exchanged between platforms or games, or traded on open markets, they will invest more of their hard-earned money." —Cointelegraph

A Galaxy Interactive pamphlet explains that as virtual products gain the same status as their physical counterparts, "The value we build inside of our virtual worlds will become indistinguishable from the value we've historically generated outside of them." NFT's integration into the gaming world is also promoting the creation of an engaging and interactive community, as players begin to create an inherent community element through peer-to-peer trading.

![](Images/image00714.jpeg)

Ranking of top ten blockchain games this past week according to NonFungible.com.

Axie Infinity, Gods Unchained, and My Crypto Heroes are three popular blockchain games that have exploded in popularity. Axie Infinity is a digital pet community where players can collect, train, raise, and battle fantasy creatures known as Axie. The game is based on the Ethereum blockchain, and it is the first blockchain game to have animated characters and rich, immersive gameplay. Gods Unchained is a digital collectible card game in which players can own and trade cards issued as NFTs with the same degree of ownership as physical cards. My Crypto Heroes is a Japanese multiplayer fighting game that uses the Ethereum blockchain to issue heroes and in-game items, allowing players to level up historical heroes through tournaments and quests.

Games should be enjoyable and fun to be successful, but Play2Earn is another crucial aspect that has important role in the widespread adoption of NFT games. Many people are attracted to the possibilities of earning money in exchange for playing a game, especially those in developing countries, where playing Axie Infinity might earn as much as a salary. Cryptopick is another example of a game where users get rewarded for their participation. Players can learn about crypto in a fun way without risking their money and, more significantly, earn their first crypto (ETH) and NFTs. Getting their first Crypto & NFTs means they'll learn about having a wallet, making a purchase, and much more, resulting in gaming being an appealing way for many to get into the Crypto world.

![](Images/image00715.jpeg)

 

Rarible is a community-driven NFT marketplace for virtually created artwork.

 

 

Art

One of the most difficult problems for digital artists is copyright infringement, but NFTs are a solution because they provide proof of ownership, authenticity, and eliminate counterfeiting and fraud issues. A post from Coindesk notes that many artists have turned to online showrooms and NFTs as galleries and museums were forced to close in the face of COVID-19, and "just as Bitcoin paved the way for trustless, peer-to-peer transactions by establishing a public ledger of events, crypto art \[...\] has provenance built-in." In July, "Picasso's Bull" set a new record for the highest-valued NFT art auction sale, selling for more than $55,000. Other records include "Portraits of a Mind" (sold for over $130,000) and "Right Place and Right Time" (sold for over $100,000). "According to Cointelegraph. These ventures can also boost and streamline artists' income by linking them directly to customers via exchange solutions and blockchain-based payment."

Examples of marketplaces and platforms for digital artwork include Makersplace, Rarible, and SuperRare. These marketplaces and platforms function similarly, allowing users to quickly create original digital artwork and sell it on their website. Rarible is a particularly fascinating example because it combines digital collectibles with yield farming and liquidity mining to create one of the most interesting DeFi trends of 2020. They were able to do so by issuing the

$RARI governance token. One of the reasons for the revived interest in NFTs in the final months of 2020 has been this adoption strategy. When users sell and trade their NFTs on the platform, they are compensated and incentivized with Rarible's governance token ($RARI). As a result, Rarible is a community-run marketplace aiming to become a completely decentralized autonomous organization (DAO), with the

$RARI token serving as the first step. Besides Rarible, many other NFT marketplaces and platforms have begun issuing and distributing governance tokens, including The Somnium Space ($CUBES), Decentraland ($MANA), Sandbox ($SAND), to name a few.

 

 

Virtual worlds

Virtual worlds are another use case for NFTs. Users can create, own, and monetize virtual land parcels, and other in- game NFT products on decentralized virtual reality platforms including The Sandbox, Cryptovoxels, and Decentraland. Decentraland's LAND is permanently owned by the community, giving players complete ownership of their virtual properties and creations. Given Gen Z's experience with virtual worlds and how their understanding of valuable assets varies from that of previous generations, virtual world assets provide them with the versatility and choice that they value: "Perhaps our children's generation won't own the house they live in, but they will own a fortune in digital assets."

![](Images/image00716.jpeg)

 

The Sandbox is another virtual world based on Blockchain and NFTs.

 

 

Real-world assets and documentation

It is possible to tokenize real-world assets such as shares and property and documents such as medical histories, qualifications, licenses, birth and death certificates. However, this category is still in its early stages of development, and

its use cases are relatively rare. But, as the crypto world and NFTs grow and expand, who's to say you won't be able to own a vineyard in another country thousands of miles away one day (perhaps soon)? Your digital wallet can soon contain proof of every license, certificate, and asset you own.

What Does the Future Hold for NFTs?

The possibilities and potential for NFTs are limitless, and recent sales records are helping in the advancement of the technology. The lack of widespread knowledge about blockchain technology and the crypto world is one of the current obstacles to its widespread adoption. "If you educate people, give them a taste of it, let them feel it, they won't be overwhelmed by it," says Grow Your Base founder John G. Fields.

Security is a part of this education. New users entering the blockchain space must know how to protect their wallets and private keys, such that their valuable digital assets are not exposed to hackers. Many digital collectibles, assets, and investments are extremely valuable due to their rarity and scarcity, and losing them will certainly be devastating. To protect the value of digital properties, developers and creators must ensure that solid licensing and copyrighting are written into smart contracts. Most crypto wallets currently available are extremely complicated and difficult to use for new users and mainstream beginners. However, to improve this wallet, such as the Coinbase wallet, Enjin wallet, Pillar wallet, or WAX Cloud Wallet (WCW), to name a few, are constantly being developed and released.

NGRAVE ZERO is another to keep an eye on because it's built to be the "coldest" and most safe crypto wallet that's also extremely user-friendly and seamless to use, giving you total peace of mind when dealing with your NFTs. ZERO's high-quality touch screen not only offers a safe and secured environment for users to store and trade their virtual properties, but it also has the intriguing potential of allowing owners to display their virtual assets and collectibles to others from a small and portable device.

Another aspect of mass education is understanding how NFTs and blockchain technology remains nerdy, geeky, and technical. Further simplification would be needed to be simple to use and easy to understand for those unfamiliar with Blockchain. In the panel ‘The Future of Gaming & Non- Fungible Tokens’, Dr. Jesse Reich (CEO Splinterlands) discusses how the difficulty "lies in making blockchain invisible to a novice user, while open to an experienced user." Although projects like CryptoKitties have successfully introduced new users to Blockchain and the growth and rise of NFT use cases in recent months, there is still a long way to go. The enthusiasm surrounding NFTs is largely concentrated in the niche areas of gaming, art, and collectibles, but new projects are steadily extending NFTs to other facets of our physical lives. We'll see how NFTs evolve to become transferable between different worlds — physical and virtual alike — as more major brands join the fray (like Nike, which has licensed shoes as NFTs called CryptoKicks enabling users to 'breed' different shoes to make a custom sneaker). As Yat Siu (CEO of Animoca Brands) so aptly puts it; "How many game companies out there would say, let's make use of those Nike shoes within our virtual games?" — Animoca Brands CEO Yat Siu As more people become aware of the opportunity and value that NFTs can provide, more big brands, venture capital companies, and major investors are taking note and getting involved. Anthony Pompliano and Jason Williams, co- founders of Morgan Creek Digital, have made a "huge bet" on digital art NFTs surpassing the physical art market.

Indie blockchain game developers have begun to attract venture capital due to their extremely long product life cycles, and willingness to deliver various monetization models, making them ideal investments. New governance tokens in the NFT sector generate interest, just as they did in the DeFi sector, and there's no telling what exciting new forms NFTs and DeFi can combine. However, the road ahead for NFTs is not without obstacles, as they may face regulatory obstacles in the future. Regardless, the crypto space is still very young and needs to continue to develop as a group by producing a large number of new projects, even though those projects come, fail, and go.

# CHAPTER 2:

 

# HOW TO MAKE MONEY WITH NFTS

 

 

 

NFTs, or non-fungible tokens, are quickly becoming one of the most lucrative blockchain-based experiments ever.

Influential people, such as Twitter CEO Jack Dorsey, have recently made headlines by auctioning off NFTs. While the cryptology behind NFTs is complex, figuring out what they are and how to profit from them is not.

This chapter will look at what an NFT is, how to sell NFTs for profit, where they're traded, and recent examples of NFT sales.

"Non-fungible token" means a crypto asset that cannot be reproduced or altered. These tokens may be thought of as the digital equivalent of artwork in a private collection. Each piece of art in the collection is one-of-a-kind, and is valued differently.

An NFT, like a piece of art, can be exchanged for cash or cryptocurrencies. However, just like cryptocurrencies, the token's asset transfer is registered in the Blockchain. This establishes who owns it.

Each NFT is linked to the original owner's digital (or, in some cases, physical) asset. Technically, any digital form can be converted into an NFT. Facebook posts, Instagram pictures, Famous tweets, and more, can be converted into NFTs and sold by anyone.

 

What Gives an NFT Value?

Consumer interest drives the value of an NFT, just as it does for items in our hypothetical art collection. The rarity and base of interested buyers affect their value, just as they do with trading cards. While a picture can be transformed into an NFT, this does not preclude it from being freely shared. The Blockchain, on the other hand, clearly shows who owns the asset.

How Can I Buy an NFT?

While some platforms accept credit cards, most sites selling and buying NFTs require you to use cryptocurrency. You will need to create a wallet for the currency you want to use. One famous platform that allows users to create wallets for free is Blockchain.com.

You can buy the currency directly around its market value after checking your identity on one of these platforms. It's time to look at an auction site once you've amassed a significant amount of a common cryptocurrency like Bitcoin (BTC).

OpenSea is a common marketplace for all kinds of NFTs. It's similar to websites like eBay that sell collectibles. You can create your digital art for a low price.

Important information for those new to cryptocurrency: You will be charged a fee when you check out with cryptocurrencies. The current state of the Blockchain determines the fee and the currency being used.

 

How Can I Show Off My NFTs?

 

Lazy.com, founded by Mark Cuban, is the first service that allows you to display your NFT collection. To prove ownership, simply create an account and follow the on-site prompts. You'll be able to show off your exclusive digital content to the rest of the world in just a few minutes!

There are other options, but Lazy.com is by far the most convenient.

 

How Can I Sell an NFT?

To begin, you'll need a unique piece of media, to which you own the copyright. This could be a photo you took, a song you wrote, a gif you created, or any other form of media.

Go to Rarible.com next. On the top-right corner of the screen, click on the "Create" button. You'll probably want to start by selling a single file, so when prompted, select "Single." After that, you need to think of a selling price for your item. You may choose how much cryptocurrency you want to use and enter it into the system.

If anyone buys your asset and uses it somewhere, you'll be asked for royalty payments. Setting a low rate equivalent to a few cents or so per view of your digital asset is optional, but it's a good idea. Keep in mind that for each item sold, Rarible retains a 2.5 percent service fee.

Finally, you will need to pay a miners' fee, also known as a "gas" fee, at the time of listing. To do so, you will need to add your newly made crypto wallet. Send the required funds, and Rarible will build a sales page for you. Your Rarible listing will automatically spread to other listing sites like OpenSea.

 

How Do I Know if My NFT Sells?

You'll need to check the sales page of the asset you’re selling for new sales offers, as these sites don't use traditional email.

After that, the crypto funds will be moved to your wallet. You'll be able to withdraw this, and use it to buy other crypto-based items, or trade it for cash.

What's the Easiest Way to Profit From NFTs?

For more complex NFTs, you should consult a professional. Since NFTs are still relatively new, your best bet right now is to recruit a freelancer through a platform like Upwork.

You can do it yourself on Rarible or a similar site for easy, single pieces of media. However, to sell more complex assets, you'll need to understand the basics of cryptocurrency, and have prior experience in the field.

You can benefit from NFTs without ever touching one! Some people treat them as if they were stocks. You may be able to make a decent profit later on if you buy an NFT of something potentially lucrative early on. Keep in mind that the opposite might, sadly, happen with your investment.

In short, the best way to profit from NFTs depends on the person. If you have extra cash, your best bet may be to purchase an asset that will appreciate over time. Although some higher-profile NFTs are currently overpriced, the NFT market is just beginning, so you'll face much less competition. If you're an influencer or content creator, you'd be better off making and selling your own original NFTs.

 

What Are Some Examples of High-Profile NFT Sales?

 

Chris Torres, the creator of the popular Nyan Cat gif, created an NFT to sell ownership of the animation. Despite the fact that Nyan Cat was first published on the internet a decade ago, Torres decided to auction it off due to a recent surge of interest in NFTs. He was able to make a profit of about

$590,000 in the cryptocurrency Ethereum.

Twitter CEO Jack Dorsey sold his first-ever tweet as an NFT, making it one of the first NFT sales to make headlines. It eventually sold for $2,500,000, with the proceeds going to charity.

Christie's auction house had the most expensive NFT auction. It was the famed house's first dive into blockchain auctions. The image was a massive JPEG file containing 5,000 separate photographs taken by a graphic designer.

Although he wasn't well-known, it sold for just under

$70,000,000. It was also the first-ever public sale of an NFT, which contributed to its success. NFTs, on the other hand, remain novel enough that "unicorn" sales like this still happen.

 

What Is the Future of NFTs?

The future of this blockchain experiment, like most others, is unknown. However, prominent publications indicate that NFTs do not seem to be going away anytime soon. Now that affluent investors are pouring money into them, it's likely they'll become more popular.

This might be the next big thing in music sales, with the ability to sell the right to use properties without giving up possession. To enter the market with the least risk, anything creative you own can be valuable. Game sprites, pictures you've taken, music you've recorded, prominent accounts on various forums and sites, and pretty much everything else you can think of could be an NFT.

# CHAPTER 3:

 

# HOW TO INVEST IN NFT ART: 5 THINGS TO KNOW

 

 

 

Since NFTs and Blockchain aren't going away anytime soon, it may be worthwhile to consider selling or purchasing NFT assets. However, there are five things you should

consider before you do so.

Global Warming

When I first learned that a single piece of crypto art is responsible for potentially hundreds of tons of greenhouse gas emissions, it didn't make sense. In my opinion, a piece of digital art can generate fewer emissions than a fine art painting, due to the necessary materials and transportation considered for the transaction or sale of physical artwork. As a result, learning about the devastating impacts NFTs have on the environment came as a shock.

The aspect of the NFT process that causes the

greatest environmental damage is the work of the miners. Miners are in charge of checking NFT transactions, and adding them to the Blockchain; they are users worldwide who attempt to solve a math problem or puzzle to add another block to the chain. (This process is called mining.)

The miner receives a "proof of work" after the

puzzle has been completed. In general, proof of work is an authentication mechanism that ensures that all transactions are safe and secure.

However, this proof of work comes at a high cost. The computers that create these puzzles and problems for miners to solve spend so much energy on outdated methods that it'd be less profitable for a hacker to try to interfere with the ledgers. According to The Verge, these Ethereum blockchain transactions consume the same amount of electricity as the entire country of Libya.

When it comes to providing NFT trading capabilities, some businesses had criticized that they had stopped doing so before they began. The website ArtStation had planned to provide a forum for artists and consumers to purchase and sell digital artwork. Still, after hearing too much about the negative environmental impact of NFTs, they decided against it.

Each consumer who buys or sells on the NFT marketplace contributes to some of these emissions, so if you don't want to contribute to the wasteful and excessive use of energy in these data mining processes, collecting crypto artwork may not be worth it to you. It's up to you to determine if the future financial gains from investing in NFTs are morally worth it in the grand scheme of things.

Electronic Ware

Crypto art, such as NFTs, is prone to wear over time, even if it seems out of reach or too far in the future. Physical pieces of art are subject to wear over time due to light exposure, which causes dullness, paint cracking, and distortion from transportation. This is why museums spend millions of dollars on providing the best possible environment for paintings.

NFTs do not have the same level of protection in the digital world. Videos, GIFs, Images, and so on, sold as NFTs can experience wear from pixilation over time, fading images, skipping content, and other ways of digital ware over time.

Consider the long-term consequences of investing in an NFT or NFT company, as well as spending valuable resources such as your time and money to create NFTs. In a few years, the crypto artwork you bought today may not be in the same pristine condition it was when you bought it.

Changing Trends

NFTs have grown in popularity in recent years, and it's difficult to tell if this trend is here to stay. Many consumers believe that the industry is only just getting started, given the current high demand for NFTs and digital assets.

However, it seems that only those who have previously invested in the crypto asset world are enthusiastic about the concept of NFTs. Although some celebrities have engaged in NFT sales, outsiders going out of their way to have a presence in the digital world would be a true sign of success. For example, suppose more conventional art collectors, venture capitalists, and everyday people incorporate digital asset investments in their portfolios. In that case, we can be more confident that NFTs will be more than a fad in the future.

If NFTs turn out to be a fad, you could lose a lot of money if you decide to invest heavily in them. So, if the value of crypto assets declines, the investor who invested $69 million

in Beeple's work, "The First 5,000 Days," will suffer a significant loss in the years ahead.

Digital Art Theft

Although blockchains are virtually impossible to hack, and ledgers are extremely unlikely to be tampered with, art theft is not entirely out of the question. Not only can ledgers and blockchains be hacked, but impersonated artwork is also a real possibility.

Artist Derek Laufman expressed his displeasure on Twitter about how convenient it is for buyers to be duped on the NFT website. He argues that this contradicts the platform's entire appeal since blockchains are supposed to have verified artists' works.

Before purchasing NFTs, it's necessary to understand the risks of blockchain ledgers that might not be advertised.

Other options

Finally, before investing in NFTs, you should consider all other options you might have for getting interested in the cryptocurrency industry, as well as various ways to do it if you do plan to do so.

You should only download the files you are interested in. This piece of advice isn't for those looking to make money from NFT transactions; rather, it's for those who genuinely enjoy acquiring digital art for their collection. Any piece of crypto art can be downloaded, but only without the token attached to the work. If you're thinking about buying an NFT because you want a dancing kitten on your home

screen, you can do so through a blockchain without any sort of hassle or purchase through a blockchain.

Use simple blockchain systems to avoid confusion. Blockchains that run under WAX are more user-friendly options for the average person interested in getting involved. To complete a transaction on more complex blockchain platforms, users must read and write code.

If you're certain you want to join the NFT market, there are a few sites to keep in mind that are more environmentally friendly than typical NFT trading sites. The NBA's Top Shot platform, for example, used the Flow Blockchain model to reduce emissions produced by each transaction.

# CHAPTER 4:

 

# HOW TO BUY AND SELL NFTS

 

 

 

The following discussion will focus on the significance of NFTs, and include an overview of how to buy and sell them.

Non-fungible tokens have recently swept the collectible and digital art worlds. All of a sudden, every digital artist is discovering new ways to showcase their work, and earn a good living doing so. Not only digital artists, but also celebrities and multinational corporations are vying for a piece of the digital asset pie. The sudden increase in popularity of non-fungible tokens (NFTs) is due to a record- breaking sale of a non-fungible token or NFT art for $69 million Ethereum.

NFTs are now being used by digital artists such as Beeple to release their work, and Blockchain will verify if the price is genuine or not. However, NFTs aren't just for digital art.

NFTs may be used in several ways, such as ownership of rare and unique items, or as another type of asset. It can also take the form of a physical object, so NFTs aren't just digital.

Investing in non-fungible tokens differs from cryptocurrency investing. Every NFT is unique, and each will have its own set of valuations and pricing. To buy and sell NFTs, some kind of media must be attached to them to increase their value. Because of all the attention paid to NFTs, many people are now confused about buying and selling them. As a result, the emphasis of this chapter will be on the process of using NFTs as a form of investment, and how to buy and sell NFTs to take advantage of this new opportunity.

 

 

How to Purchase NFT

If you want to learn how to buy NFT, there are a few steps you must take. Before you can purchase any NFTs, you must first determine where they are being sold. There are marketplaces where you can buy and sell NFTs since it is a blockchain-based token. So, before we go any further, let's take a look at some of the most famous marketplaces.

Where to Buy NFTs

You can buy and sell NFTs on several different online marketplaces. However, not all marketplaces sell the same collectibles or works of art. As a result, you can buy a specific type of collectible depending on the marketplace you choose. Most of these marketplaces have a large selection of NFTs to choose from, but every platform operates differently.

If you're confused about where to purchase NFTs, here are some of the marketplaces where you can buy and sell them:

OpenSea

OpenSea is a fantastic Ethereum-based NFT marketplace. Users can also use crypto to purchase non-fungible tokens. It has a wide range of collectibles, from artwork to video games. You will, however, need a blockchain wallet such as Metamask to use this platform. OpenSea supports many blockchain wallets such as Trust, Coinbase, Metamask, Argent, and so on. This is one of the most popular platforms to buy NFTs.

SuperRare

Another decent place to buy NFTs is SuperRare. All of the items are unique, and people can sell and buy tokens through their platforms. Furthermore, since this platform is based on the Ethereum network, you will need to purchase Ether to purchase on this site.

CryptoPunks

CryptoPunks is currently one of the rising stars in the industry. It differs from other NFT marketplaces in a few ways. These are mainly 24×24 pixel images. All of the images are automatically generated using algorithms. Most of the pictures are of girls and boys dressed as punks, but there are a few exceptions, such as alien punks and zombie punks. In either case, each punk has their portfolio where you can view their ownership status, characteristics, and whether or not they are for sale.

This is similar to CryptoKitties, where each item is unique. Similarly, every punk is unique. You can buy punks, bid on them, and then sell them for a higher price in this marketplace.

Rarible

Rarible is a token-based blockchain digital transformation platform that is similar to OpenSea. You can also make, purchase, sell, and bid on art pieces in this marketplace. All you have to do is upload your digital content to this website, create an NFT, and auction it. People who want to buy these digital images can place bids on them, and the image goes to the highest bidder. You can also create several NFTs for a single picture for sale purposes, and sell it multiple times.

Furthermore, you might be paid a commission on resales. Sorare

Sorare is designed especially for soccer cards. This website allows you to buy and sell limited-edition digital soccer cards. Currently, there are 125 clubs listed in the marketplace, with more being added every week. If you're a soccer fan, Sorare is the NFT marketplace for you so that you can purchase your favorite players' soccer cards.

Creating and Funding Your Account

You can create an account on any NFT marketplace. However, you must first link to your blockchain wallet before you can buy or sell anything. Since most NFTs are Ethereum-based, most marketplaces recognize Ether as a form of payment. You'll need a crypto exchange account to buy Ether, and then you'll need to upload the cryptocurrency to your blockchain wallet.

You can connect your wallet to your NFT marketplace account once you have enough funds. There, you are done! You can now purchase NFT whenever you want!

Blockchain Wallets

To buy and sell NFTs, you'll need a blockchain wallet that supports ERC standards like ERC-721 and ERC-1155, as you probably already know. Both of these are designed specifically for NFTs. However, since ERC-1155 is relatively new, some popular wallets may not yet support it.

To help you, we've compiled a list of blockchain wallets that you can use to store your NFTs and other cryptos to purchase NFT. Most NFT marketplaces support these wallets.

MetaMask

MetaMask is one of the most common Ethereum-based decentralized finance applications. You can use this wallet as a browser extension for Firefox, Chrome, and other browsers. Metamask allows you to access your funds as well as other Ethereum-based tokens.

If you're a blockchain developer, MetaMask also reduces the barrier to entry for people interested in Ethereum by providing an abstraction to users. This is also where you can keep your NFTs. Furthermore, the device stores and encrypts all of your private keys. You have access to the keys at any time and can accept or reject NFT sales.

Coinbase Wallet

The Coinbase wallet is another popular option among the NFT platforms. It supports several coins, as well as other types of digital collectibles, and provides safe storage. There is one significant difference here. Instead of using only addresses, you can use your Coinbase wallet name to make transactions. It is available as a standalone app, making it even more convenient to use because you can install it on your mobile devices.

 

## Buy Your NFT

This is the last step you need to take to purchase NFT. The purchasing process is simple once you've funded your wallet and account. Most NFT marketplaces have an auction mode, in which you can place a bid and wait a given

period to see if you got the piece. An auction can last for days, and you'll have to outbid the highest bidder if you want to buy a piece.

Buying an NFT from the primary marketplace, on the other hand, is a good idea because the resale value is very high when you sell it on another website. However, there

are some disadvantages to it. In any case, it's difficult to predict how the NFT will be evaluated because demand can fluctuate at any time.

Now that you've learned how to buy NFT, it's time to learn how to make and sell NFT on these platforms.

 

 

How to Create and Sell NFTs

You can go two different paths to sell NFT. To begin, you can purchase an existing NFT piece and then resell it on another platform. Second, you can make your own NFTs, auction them, and sell them on other platforms. The process of purchasing NFTs has already been discussed. But now, we'll talk about how you can create your own NFT.

 

 

How Are NFTs Created?

Creating NFT is a very simple and straightforward procedure. You'll need to register with a marketplace that allows you to create NFTs. Users can purchase, sell, and even build NFTs on marketplaces like Rarible or OpenSea. The best part is that the ERC-721 tokens can be generated without any prior knowledge of Blockchain or cryptographic hashing.

This platform will convert your video, image, 3D models, or any other asset. It's not the same as asset tokenization. Well, for an NFT to be valuable, the media must be connected in some way. The reputation of an artist is what gives an NFT the value it deserves.

 

## How Much Does It Cost?

Some platforms will usually charge you a small fee for creating NFTs. In Opensea, however, there are no costs associated with creating an NFT. There is just a one-time gas fee for making the first NFT, and everything else is free. If the marketplace uses Ethereum NFTs, you'll need "gas" to create the tokens.

Ethereum gas cost can vary depending on the network. The higher the gas price, the more people there are on the network. We recommend trying it out on the weekend because there are less users on the network.

Can Anyone Make An NFT?

There are no restrictions on who can make an NFT, or gain access to the marketplace. It's open for everybody. An account is all that is needed to buy or sell NFTs. Buying is a lot easier than selling because you already know how much the token is worth. When you're making your own NFTs, though, the valuation isn't the same.

Not everybody can go out and create an NFT and expect a decent return on their investment. It's a little like how real- life artists work. It is more difficult to sell if you are not popular.

However, the hidden fees in various sites can become very complicated. Even if you can sell one, the hidden fees will often amount to more tokens than you are receiving in your sales. As a result, you'll have to sell your tokens at a loss in this case. We recommend doing extensive research on the platforms and building a fanbase before you start minting your NFTs.

The Process of Selling NFTs

It's time to understand how to cash in on your NFT collections. To sell NFT, log into your account and look for the item in your collections. Once you've located them, simply click on them to reveal a "sell" button. Once you've found this option, click it to go to another page where you can set the auction's pricing and conditions.

The process is slightly different in all marketplaces. If you sell your NFT, one of the most popular tokens you'll get is Ether, also known as ERC-20. Platforms such as Opensea offers a royalty fee in exchange for a commission each time the original work is sold.

Now that you know how to create and sell NFTs, you can begin your endeavors anytime you want.

# CHAPTER 5:

 

# THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN FUNGIBLE AND NON- FUNGIBLE TOKENS

 

 

The following will clarify the controversy between fungible and non-fungible tokens, and help you understand the differences between the two.

Cryptocurrencies have always been connected to blockchain technology. If you're new to the world of Blockchain, you're probably under the impression that it's just great for developing crypto assets. The real potential of blockchain technology, on the other hand, goes far beyond cryptocurrencies.

Governments, businesses, and individuals can now place real estate data, IDs, certificates, and other critical information about real-world assets on Blockchain. Students could easily have their certificates issued as a digital document on the Blockchain, which authorities will accept all over the world. So, what has

helped people accomplish such a remarkable feat?

This is where the controversy about fungible and non-fungible tokens has found recognition in recent years. Non-fungible tokens are being heralded as the future of blockchain economy. However, fungible tokens, or current cryptocurrencies are often pitted against them. Let's clear up any confusion by examining the distinction between fungible and non-fungible tokens.

 

What Are Tokens?

The definition of tokens is the first thing you should consider when determining the differences between fungible and non-fungible tokens. Tokens are a tangible or visible representation of a fact, feeling, or quality in the real world. People come across a variety of tokens in real life.

A hotel key card, for example, is evidence of payment to the hotel for a room. The office ID card serves as evidence of your employment in a company. On the other hand, a driving license certifies that a person has undergone the necessary training to drive in their country. As a result, a token also represents a particular entity in the crypto world.

The token can hold value, stakes, voting rights, or anything. Surprisingly, a token is not limited to a single role, and can be used to address various roles in its native ecosystem. A token may be used to reflect a company's utility or asset. The company can give the tokens to investors in a public sale.

## The Importance of Tokens

So, what is the role of a token? The response to this question is crucial to fully comprehend the fungible vs. non-fungible token debate. A token isn't limited to a particular role and can be used for various things in its native ecosystem. The following are examples of possible functions:

1. Tokens may be used as entry points into blockchain applications, and users must have tokens to use the decentralized app.

2. Tokens may also represent an individual's eligibility to retain special voting rights. EOS blockchain tokens, for example, can be used to vote for block creators.

3.           Tokens may also be useful entities for improving holders' user experiences. Enhancement tokens for user experience can help improve the user experience within the confines of a particular environment. For example, in the case of users of BAT, the Brave web browser or other Brave-related tokens can enhance their consumer experience by using their tokens to add adverts or other attention-based services related to the Brave platform.

4.           Most importantly, the use of tokens as currency aids in identifying fungible and non-fungible tokens. Tokens can be used as a store of value for internal and external transactions in a given ecosystem, and they have a unique monetary structure that includes digital assets.

5.           Another vital application of tokens refers to value exchange. In fact, in the blockchain ecosystem, tokens have historically been used for value exchange. As a result, tokens can be useful in developing an internal economic system in an application.

6.           It's also important to consider the application of tokens as a means of identifying who owns a specific entity. Tokens may signify ownership of something unique to a single person, and this application lays the groundwork for the debate over fungible vs. non-fungible tokens.

 

## Foundation for Comparing Fungible and Non-Fungible Tokens

Blockchain is the best technology for managing different forms of digital assets. Its security and immutability features make it suitable for handling digital assets. It is difficult to write anyunique information to fungible tokens, which are the most widely used ones in Blockchain.

 

Cryptographic tokens, on the other hand, which are unique and can hold data rather than value, could be the best option. Non-fungible tokens are those that are defined on the Ethereum platform using ERC standards like the ERC-721 standard. The distinction between fungible and non-fungible tokens emerges from the reliance on data

storage. Let's establish the basis for comparing fungible and non-fungible tokens by reflecting on their definitions.

In economic terms, any fungible entity refers to an asset's or good's capacity to be interchanged with another asset or good of equal value. Currency and money are the most popular examples of fungible assets. For example, a $5 bill in one person's possession has the same value as a $5 bill in another person's possession. Similarly, two $5 bills of one person are worth the same as a single $10 bill of another.

Gold is another fungible asset since one ounce of gold in one country has the same value as one ounce of gold in another. The debate about fungible vs. non-fungible tokens stems from the fact that fungibility is written into the Bitcoin and other cryptocurrency code. The most important feature of a fungible good is that it is standardized, and its units are not unique.

NFTs, on the other hand, act as a special token variant with no interchangeability with other tokens, as discussed in the debate between fungible and non-fungible tokens. NFT has distinct characteristics that indicate that it cannot be replaced or exchanged for another token of the same kind.

Non-fungible tokens vary from cryptocurrencies in that they have no inherent value. Non-fungible tokens derive their value from the goods or assets represented by them. NFTs use a variety of token standards and deploy several types

of smart contract. Non-fungible tokens are seen as new- age tools for developing a blockchain-based virtual economic ecosystem.

Explicit Differences between Fungible and Non-Fungible Tokens

Now that we have a good understanding of what fungible and non-fungible tokens are, it's time to focus on comparing fungible and non-fungible tokens. Let us consider the qualities that differentiate fungible and non- fungible tokens.

Interchangeability

Fungible tokens, as you must have understood, are completely interchangeable with each other. Fiat currencies are the most well-known example of fungible assets. You can transfer fungible assets from one owner to another to make payments for such items. Exchanging fungible assets, on the other hand, has no meaning because they have the same value. Fungible tokens are generally used for tracking balances and making payments.

One of the most popular examples of practical blockchain use cases of fungible tokens is binding fungible tokens to an organization's account balance in a specific payment account. As a result, payments could be completed with token

transfers. Users may also query the resulting token balances to perform periodic netting and settlement.

Value Transfer

Another important aspect is the distinction between fungible and non-fungible tokens, which relates to the fact that each account maintains a balance based on the tokens owned by the account. It is quite easy to transfer tokens to other Ethereum accounts using swap techniques or direct transactions. When using token transfers, the source account is debited with the transfer amount, just like when using bank transfers. At the same time, the beneficiary account receives a credit of the same amount.

The fungible vs. non-fungible tokens comparison means that non-fungible tokens or NFTs have a distinct value proposition. NFTs are unique tokens, and each one has a unique ID to distinguish it from other tokens in the same smart contract. The non-fungible token always has a unique owner. As a result, their values can vary because each token is treated differently.

Certain NFTs will represent unique tributes with a proven scarcity. As a result, NFTs are in high demand, with more buyers and higher value than those representing

common attributes. Furthermore, NFTs can be used to facilitate both ownership transfer and trading. For example, John might exchange tokens representing a batch of Scottish whiskey for tokens representing a batch of Bordeaux wine.

One of the most notable use cases is assigning a non- fungible token to a real estate property. After paying off the mortgage, users can transfer NFTs from the bank's Ethereum account to the house owner's Ethereum account. Therefore, it introduces a new form of use-case for Blockchain in the real estate sector.

 

 

Ethereum Token Standards

The next significant factor to consider when choosing between fungible and non-fungible tokens refers to token standards. If you want to build a healthy ecosystem, you can build decentralized apps on top of Ethereum that can interact with one another seamlessly. However, if you have two different tokens, such as Token Alpha and Token Beta, can you find different smart contract structures in them?

Developers must study both contracts for the interaction between the two tokens.

Furthermore, developers must map out the precise methods by which tokens will interact with one another. This factor, however, does not help with scalability. If there are 100 different tokens with 100 different smart contracts, it would be difficult to narrow down all qualifications and conditions for ensuring that transfers will go through all tokens.

As a result, you can encounter a large number of complex calculations for token transactions that could be encountered. Thus, token standards evolved as a viable option for distinguishing between fungible and non-fungible tokens. So, the decision to standardize the rules governing the underlying architecture of tokens is unquestionably a yardstick for distinguishing fungible from non-fungible tokens.

 

 

Fungible Tokens Use ERC-20

ERC-20 is the specific set of rules, and ERC stands for 'Ethereum Request for Comment.' The foundations of ERC- 20 depend on elements such as approval, allowance, totalSupply, transfer, transferFrom, and balanceOf.

These elements are the mandatory functions and rules for ERC-20 tokens.

Tokens, on the other hand, may have the three optional characteristics mentioned below. A token name, a symbol, and a decimal up to 18 are among the features. These characteristics define the ERC-20 as a fungible standard. As a result of the standards, fungible tokens must have the following properties, which determine their position in the fungible vs. non-fungible token debate.

1.  Fungible tokens of the same type can replace one token.

2.  Similar underlying rules serve governance of fungible tokens.

3.  Fungible tokens are easily divisible, and people can use smaller fractions to pay back a larger amount.

Non-Fungible Tokens Use ERC-721

However, ERC-20's limitations result in significant inconsistencies, prompting the creation of the ERC-721 token standard. The ERC-721 token standard may be useful in the creation of non-fungible tokens. Surprisingly, the ERC-721 token standard is very similar to the ERC-20 token standard in terms of functionality. The similarities between ERC-20 and ERC-721 are mainly due to the following factors:

1.  Developers could quickly transition without having to learn a new set of skills.

2.  Users may have a better experience storing tokens in general wallets and trading them on exchanges.

The ERC-721 interface has two different methods, such as ownerOf and transferFrom, which have distinct functions. The ownerOf feature is used to find out who owns a token. The transferFrom function can be used to transfer token ownership.

# CHAPTER 6:

 

# NFTS, USABILITY AND TRENDS

 

 

 

The popularity of NFT has been increasing. From various trends to usability. The community has been very innovative in providing a diverse range of NFTs, and making them more approachable for newcomers to the Blockchain space. However, according to some statistics, only 47% of crypto users had learned of NFTs, and 57% had never used them.

The most common NFTs were for collectibles (47 percent) and gaming (33 percent), according to a survey of nearly 30k people.

What do these stats tell us? That few people are comfortable engaging with NFTs, and that general knowledge about them is still restricted. The aim remains to bring more people into the space to better understand Blockchain, and accelerate its development.

We must remain curious and pursue new projects to help space evolve, and understand all of its functions to develop products for our users better.

 

 

## A Little about NFTs

These are the most common properties of NFTs, and it's important to remember them so we can understand what we can do with them, and how they work:

 

Unique — Each NFT has its own set of properties, usually stored in the token's metadata.

Scarce is probably a small number of NFTs, with the most extreme example having just one copy, and the number of tokens can be verified on the Blockchain, hence

its provability.

Indivisible — Most NFTs can't be divided into smaller denominations, so you won't be able to purchase or transfer a fraction of your NFT.

Similar to fungible tokens, NFTs ensure asset ownership, are easily transferable, and are fraud-resistant.

The popularity of DeFi has recently aided the growth of the topic around NFTs.

How can NFTs be used in DeFi?

NFTs are being regarded as the next big thing in the Decentralized finance universe. They can be used as collateral; they may also represent other financial assets such as bonds, insurance, or options.

A DeFi borrowing and lending platform require collateral. This is where NFTs come into play to solve the problem.

Yinsure uses NFTs in the insurance space; each contract is represented as an NFT, which can then be traded on another platform (e.g., Rarible).

Another DeFi trend we have seen using NFTs is the issuance of governance tokens. Many sites and NFT marketplaces have begun to issue and distribute governance tokens.

# CHAPTER 7:

 

# HOW BRANDS ARE USING NFTS

 

 

 

Technology has advanced in recent years.

Blockchain is one area that has seen some of the most rapid advancements.

That does not mean cryptocurrencies like Ethereum, Bitcoin, and the slew of other cryptocurrencies own the crypto market solely.

Let's take a look at non-fungible tokens (NFTs) and how brands can use them.

NFTs differ from cryptocurrencies in that they have specific identification codes and metadata that distinguish one NFT from another. Cryptocurrencies are fungible tokens because they are all identical.

Because each NFT is unique, it cannot be exchanged or traded for an equal NFT. As a result, each NFT is a digital collectible, a unique, non-replicable asset.
