# CHAPTER 13:

 

# BEST NFT MARKETPLACES

 

 

In a nutshell, NFTs (non-fungible tokens) are

unique crypto tied to digital content such as artwork.

Several new NFT marketplaces have popped up, allowing users to buy and sell NFTs.

NFTs have been in existence for a while, but 2021 was the year they made a big splash into public consciousness.

The cryptographically tokens represent a title of ownership over digital property such as art, films, or music—but it was the digital art scene that started the NFT market, with record-breaking sales such as Beeple's 69 million dollars Christie's auction grabbing global headlines.

Fans have pounced on every new NFT craze since then, spending hundreds (if not millions) of dollars on games like Loot, Bored Apes, and CryptoPunks.

But, you might ask, where are these NFTs purchased? NFT marketplaces have sprouted up like a vibrant virtual high street, selling digital art and collectibles at every price point imaginable.

 

1.  OpenSea

 

The first and largest peer-to-peer NFT marketplace was OpenSea. It has a total transaction volume of more than $6.5 billion at the time of writing, allowing NFTs of everything from artwork to collectibles and game items, to GIFs, music, and more.

The fastest way to sign up is to connect your MetaMask wallet; however, alternative wallets such as Formatic, Bitski, Coinbase Wallet, and others are also supported.

Purchasing an NFT is a simple process once you've connected; all you have to do is search for something that catches your attention or browse through the collections, then submit an offer and wait to see whether it's approved.

Uploading your NFT work is likewise simple. Simply go to the "Create" tab, add your wallet as a creator, upload your NFT, provide a description, and wait for the millions to flow in.

 

2.  Axie Marketplace

 

The NFT-powered video game Axie Infinity hosts the second-largest NFT marketplace, with a cumulative trade volume of over $2.1 billion on Dappradar. It deals with Axies, charming Pokémon-style digital pets that players can purchase and trade on the Axie Marketplace.

 

 

Axie Infinity

What is Axie Infinity? The Crypto-Based Play-to-Earn NFT Game By Storm Crypto includes assets such as artwork and movies based on non-fungible tokens (NFTs). It erupted in popularity in early 2021. However, some blockchain-based video games had already been developing in the field before that...

You can also make new Axies using the game's built-in breeding mechanics, which you can sell on the Marketplace. Unlike art NFTs, which are purchased solely for the pleasure of collecting, The NFTs in Axie Infinity have a purpose: they may be used to battle beasts and other players in the game, earning tokens that can be used to create new species. The NFTs developed by Axie Infinity have proven to be so successful that some players in the Philippines and Indonesia have made a living breeding and trading them.

For novice users, Axie Infinity is one of the more challenging NFT services to utilize, and you’ll have to jump through some hoops before you can even start playing.

You will need to set up a Ronin wallet, transfer ETH into Ronin, and buy at least three Axies on the Axie market, in addition to an Ethereum wallet like MetaMask. This is not ideal for casual passers-by; however, it is not beyond the capability of a seasoned crypto user.

 

 

3.  CryptoPunks/Larva Labs

 

CryptoPunks was one of the initial examples of NFTs on the Ethereum network, a sequence of 10,000 randomly produced figures with pixel art aesthetics and different features. While they were originally freely available, they are now exclusively available for purchase.

What Are CryptoPunks? NFT Sensation in Ethereum

The market for crypto collectibles exploded once non- fungible tokens (NFTs) became widespread in 2021, with

$2.5 billion in transaction activity in the first 6 months.

That implies going to Larva Labs, CryptoPunks' founder's marketplace, where the majority of purchases are made. The most valuable punk sold on the marketplace (number 3100) went for a staggering $7.58 million, while the cheapest one currently costs 94.99 ETH (approx. $285,000). That explains Larva Labs' total trading volume of 1.3 billion USD.

To begin, simply connect your MetaMask wallet, browse the available Punks (those with red backgrounds are for sale), and place your bid.

The most challenging aspect of the procedure is persuading yourself to spend such a large sum of money, but hey, that's on you. After all, the value of art is in the eye beholder.

 

 

4.  NBA Top Shot Marketplace

 

NBA Top Shot is a digital trading card collection centered on NBA video highlights that was one of the earliest NFT series to find momentum with the general public. When you purchase a pack, the clips are kept in your secure, encrypted blockchain-verified wallet, which you may access to view or sell on the NBA Top Shot Marketplace.

The popularity of NBA Top Shot might be attributed to the fact that its makers, Dapper Labs, made it simple to get started. Connecting your Google account to Dapper is the simplest method to join up for NBA Top Shot, after which

you will be asked to provide your mobile number for SMS authentication.

After that, you'll have to go through a few account-creation steps, including selecting your preferred team. After that, you can join a new pack drop, which will place you in a simulated queue before you can grab a pack. After another SMS verification, you can pay using a Flow wallet, your Dapper balance, an existing crypto wallet, or, more easily, a credit card.

5.  Rarible

 

Rarible is a community-run website that sells a variety of digital art and collectibles. It's one of Ethereum's most popular NFT markets. With $210 million in trading volume, Dappradar is currently the fifth-highest all-time trading volume.

Just like on OpenSea, you can purchase and sell any kind of media. Sellers can also create several NFTs for a single image to sell it more than once.

For those who have dabbled in similar NFT markets like OpenSea, will feel at home with Rarible's user interface, which is simple for relative crypto rookies to operate.

You can log in using MyEtherWallet, MetaMask, Coinbase Wallet, or any other WalletConnect-enabled mobile wallet. You can use the funds in your wallet to buy, or you can top up your wallet with fiat currency through bank transfer or debit card once you've signed up.

 

6.  SuperRare

 

While Rarible's design overwhelms you with a dizzying array of flickering GIFs and live auctions, SuperRare's UI is far more pared-back and simple.

It's also a lot more curated, since it describes itself as a social network dedicated to the creation and acquisition of crypto art. It works closely with artists, requiring submission and approval of work before it can be offered; in other words, quality above quantity.

A supported wallet, such as Formatic or MetaMask, is required to sign up to SuperRare. You will need to create a username and password connected to your wallet address as a second step, but it just takes a few seconds, and you will be browsing the platform's unique NFTs in no time.

 

 

7.  KnownOrigin

 

For the discriminating NFT aficionado, KnownOrigin, like SuperRare, promises to give a more curated, gallery-like experience. Its total transaction volume is below $6.9 million. All of its artwork files are kept on IPFS, which provides some security for the underlying assets. With a significant emphasis on digital art, this is a marketplace that ignores the wackier aspects of the NFT universe; as a result, there will be no weird avatars or adorable creatures here.

KnownOrigin is quite easy to use, at the risk of sounding like a broken record. Simply link your wallets, such as

Formatic or MetaMask, and you will be bidding on your desired NFT assets in no time.

 

 

 

8.  Foundation

 

Foundation has a total trading volume of slightly over $79 million, describing itself as a "creative playground" for artists. There has been the NFT of popular Internet meme Nyan Cat, Edward Snowden's first NFT, and an audiovisual digital collectable created by producer Richard D. James, best known as Aphex Twin.

Foundation's marketplace is easy to use once you've connected your MetaMask or other software wallet

using WalletConnect. You can place bids on timed auctions like you would on a regular auction site.

 

9.  MakersPlace

 

MakersPlace is yet another NFT marketplace that has a number of unique digital fine art collections. Despite its small size ($23.5 million at the time of writing), it features a number of unique artifacts that add to its exclusivity. The site went down in February 2021 when legendary crypto artist Beeple offered a collection of NFTs for $1 each. The platform has hosted several NFT drops by singers. The likes of Shakira, T-Pain, and Tom Morello of Rage Against the Machine have signed up.

Creating an account on MakersPlace is straightforward, and you can save time by checking in with your Google or Facebook accounts.

You'll have to pick five artists to follow as part of the registration process, and you will be able to buy their works using either your MetaMask ETH balance or (more easily) your credit card. If you are a creator, you must complete an online form to seek an invitation to join the platform. This will be reviewed by the curators.

 

 

10.  Nifty Gateway

 

One of the first wave of massive NFT marketplaces, Nifty Gateway, has a key backer in cryptocurrency exchange Gemini, which bought the site in 2019. Nifty Gateway made news during the NFT surge by brokering the $6.6 million sale of Beeple's CROSSROAD in February 2021, one of the first multimillion-dollar NFT deals. By the end of May, the platform's gross revenues had surpassed $300 million.

The platform features artists such as Grimes, The Weeknd, and Eminem, as well as a carefully curated collection of NFT releases that are released on a tri-weekly basis. There's also a marketplace where you may browse verified artists, curated collections, and a bigger selection of work from unconfirmed artists.

Getting started is a relatively simple procedure thanks to Nifty Gateway's direct connection with Gemini. To get started, you'll need to sign up for an account on the website (and use Stripe to validate your identity if you wish to sell

NFTs). You will need to fund your account by sending ETH to a deposit address or a credit card. After that, you can go to the marketplace and buy NFTs.

Also, you can link your Gemini exchange account to your Nifty Gateway account, enabling you to utilize your Gemini exchange account balance to make withdrawals and purchases.

 

 

11.  BakerySwap

 

BakerySwap is a lesser NFT market than OpenSea, which is understandable given that most NFT markets are based on Ethereum, whereas BakerySwap was one of the first to launch on Binance Smart Chain (BSC). The NFT marketplace is one aspect of the platform that allows users to trade and exchange liquidity assets openly with one another.

The NFT marketplace is quite easy to use and simply requires a MetaMask connection. NFT assets can only be purchased with BNB rather than the more commonly utilized ETH because this platform is based on the Binance Smart Chain.

 

12.  Binance NFT Marketplace

 

Binance NFT marketplace is an NFT platform hosted by Binance, the world's largest crypto exchange, as you might think. To draw the exchange's enormous user base to its platform, the company plans to develop an NFT marketplace with exclusive offers and collaborations.

If you already have a Binance account, you will be pleased to learn that it will automatically work with Binance NFT. Once you've gotten in, it's as simple as bidding on any products that catch your attention. You can use BNB, BUSD, or ETH, depending on what the developer have listed.

 

 

13.  Solanart

 

The most recent NFT mini-boom took place on Solana, a competing blockchain to Ethereum. Collections like Degenerate Ape Academy, SolPunks, and Aurory have seen trading volumes of millions of dollars on platforms like Solanart.

Solanart is a curated collection of NFT sets with a restricted number of NFTs for purchase.

To start buying Solana NFTs on Solanart, you'll need to download a Solana wallet like Solflare or Phantom, and load it with Solana from your preferred exchange. Phantom features a direct integration with the cryptocurrency exchange FTX, making it simple to deposit funds from that exchange.

It's as simple as going to your chosen NFT, connecting your Solana wallet to the marketplace, then placing a bid from there.

# CHAPTER 14:

 

# SUCCESS STORY

 

 

The NFT craze has been life changing for artists like Trevor Jones, who is partnering with Ice Cube and may now purchase a Tesla.

Trevor Jones has shattered many crypto art records in the past year and a half. He has exceeded the highest bids on NFT platforms such as Nifty Gateway, MakersPlace, and SuperRare. His Bitcoin Angel broke the record for the "highest expensive open-edition NFT artwork" when it sold for $3.2 million in February.

Jones, 51, didn't attend college until he was in his thirties, and funded his painting career by working two to three jobs after graduation. Jones and his wife continue to live just outside of Edinburgh, Scotland, in relative peace. He describes his time in the NFT art community as "a crazy success story."

He continues, "It went from being depressed at 30 and intending to pursue an art degree to \[becoming\] an overnight sensation." His success shows how the recent NFT boom has changed the lives of artists. NFTs have made some people millions – in the exclusive art world, no less – and are far from an ephemeral internet phenomenon.

Jones is now one of the most important artists minting NFTs since his introduction into "crypto art" in 2018, when he used augmented reality to morph paintings at the Scottish National Portrait Gallery into crypto bigwigs. His name is well-known in the industry. He's collaborated with Pak, whose work was recently auctioned for $16.8 million at Sotheby's. When Beeple, who auctioned an NFT for $69.3 million at Christie's in March 2021, first sought Jones for help on how to make drops on Nifty Gateway last summer, Jones was too busy to respond (they later discussed partnership). His next collaboration will be Ice Cube, a rapper and filmmaker.

As the hype around NFTs rises, Jones continues to stand out as a classically educated artist in a digital age. Although, many of his works contain technology aspects (such as augmented reality, animation, and, of course, NFTs), oil on canvas remains his favorite medium. Meanwhile, he's been following the crypto art world's quick development. It mirrors the conventional art atmosphere in which he grew up in certain aspects.

"Back in the day...there were only a handful of \[crypto\] artists." "We all talked to one other on a daily basis and became enthralled with who was doing what," he recalls. "It's really blown up in the last six months. It reflects the art industry as a whole, with egos, money, and personalities, and everyone vying for a piece of the pie."

Jones excelled in art class as a child growing up in a "small logging community" in Western Canada, but his desire was to be a rock star. When things went "horribly wrong," he started traveling around the world, and in his early thirties, he found himself "at a crossroads."

"It's all pretty cliché," he adds, calling over Zoom in April from an unadorned area in his home. "I determined that in order to save myself and find atonement, I had to become an artist."

Jones graduated from Edinburgh University, and Edinburgh College of Art several years later, at the age of 38. He was initially hired as the director of the non-profit Art in Healthcare. Jones was recruited in 2008 because of his "practical skill set," which included financial expertise as well as creative ability, according to Margaret Hurcombe, who recruited him. While working on solo exhibitions, Jones supplemented his income by teaching art classes and renting out spare rooms in his home.

He quickly recognized that solo gigs wouldn't be sufficient to cover his expenses. He was left with roughly £9,000 from an exhibition that took a year to put up, thanks to gallery commissions and overhead costs like framing.

Jones' work is valuable to collectors. "I started looking at it objectively and sensibly," Jones explains. He needed to carve out a niche for himself in his early forties to set him apart from other artists. As a result, he turned to technology, painting QR codes in 2012, and dabbling in augmented reality in 2013.

Jones showed up with "traditional looking" animal and landscape paintings during the 2013 Edinburgh Art Fair, according to Andy McDougall, the fair's founder and contemporary art dealer. "This abrupt shift in style perplexed us all”, McDougall says, until Jones brought out his iPad and scanned his painting, which began to move, play music, and offer information about its subject. "He was able to bring the paintings to life," adds McDougall.

Jones used AR to recreate Vladimir Putin and Donald Trump in a "global leaders" portrait series from 2016, combining paint with garbage to portray them. All ten paintings were purchased by one buyer. "I had a little money in the bank for the first time," Jones says, "and I could look into investing."

His timing was fateful. Around 2017, the values of ether (ETH) and bitcoin (BTC) skyrocketed. Jones became engrossed in the stock market. "I discovered I'm a bit of a gambler," he admits. His "investment" efforts failed miserably.

However, they did introduce him to a new subject matter: the crypto world, which had previously been

explored by traditional artists. He developed his stealth AR display at the Scottish National Portrait Gallery, painting portraits of John McAfee (who retweeted his work) and Vitalik Buterin. By dedicating so much time to work in this unexplored territory, Jones had taken yet another risk. This time, the risk paid off.

As more people began to follow Jones on Twitter, where he has no issues with self-promotion, interest in his work expanded throughout the crypto community. By 2019, he started painting portraits for this publication's "Most Influential" series. "That established my status as the space's go-to traditional painter," he says.

Despite the fact that NFTs had been around for a while, Jones didn't learn about them until he met David Moore, the CEO of digital art marketplace KnownOrigin, at Manchester's CoinFest in April 2019. Jones explains, "He was telling me about NFTs, and I could not really wrap my head around it at all."

A few months later, Jones discovered artists selling their paintings on KnownOrigin. He says, "I realized I could not ignore this any longer." So he texted a French artist by the name Alotta Money, whose NFTs were fetching hundreds of dollars.

Alotta Money says, "Everybody in the NFT wanted Trevor in." "He came to me and inquired if I could help him”.

EthGirl is an animated girl in the style of the Ethereum sign that separates to reveal Picasso's pixelated eye, which was inspired by Picasso's Girl with Mandolin (1910). It shattered SuperRare's sale record when it sold for 72.1 ETH in December 2019. "Watch this space, this is just the beginning" says Jones in his SuperRare description of the item. He was absolutely correct.

NFT art marketplaces like Nifty Gateway were launched the next year, broadening the audience for NFTs by enabling buyers to pay in fiat currency. Jones set a new platform record in July when he sold Picasso's Bull for $55,555.

Grimes' NFT artwork sold for $5.8 million, Azealia Banks' audio sex tape sold for $17,240, Chris Crocker, the artist behind the "Leave Britney Alone" film, sold for more than

$44,000 Christie's and Sotheby's had multi-million dollar NFT auctions in 2021.

According to Jones, many people who have shown interest in NFTs since last summer "credit Picasso's Bull NFT as the one that drew them in." By the end of 2020, Jones had released his second collaboration with Alotta Money, which had sold for about $140,000. Jones had no idea who Pak was, thus his collaboration with him was worth $1.4 million.

![](Images/image00720.jpeg)

EthBoy, 2019, 140 x 110 cm, oil & wax on canvas

Due to his history in the legacy art field, Jones could see the NFT business exploding before it happened. "I knew there would be a Beeple who'd come in and really open everything up," he adds. I just didn't expect it to happen so quickly."

The market's rise, according to Jones, is both "positive and negative." Emerging artists have more attention and money at their disposal, and they can make more money from their art because NFT platforms pay royalties on resales, and take less commision than established galleries. It does make it more challenging for young or lesser-known performers to gain recognition.

Jones appreciates assisting industry beginners. Aisha Arif, manager at MakersPlace, a digital art marketplace, thinks Trevor “is a role model”. He is always offering helpful ideas to help artists on Twitter."

He is, nevertheless, inundated with emails these days. He is overwhelmed by the volume of inquiries and plans to hire an assistant as soon as is feasible.

Despite his success, Jones has remained humble, friendly, and a fantastic friend, according to friends and coworkers. It's made him more guarded, if anything. The pessimism that may pervade the crypto sector reaches sensitive artists, and Jones admits that he has been tempted to "totally step away" several times in the last year. He instead focused on his work.

After all, components of blockchain technology ,such as the Metaverse, where artists can showcase their NFT work in virtual environments, excite him from an aesthetic sense and will "completely revolutionize the way art is consumed," he argues.

Jones' surprising fortune remains "a number on a screen" (really, multiple numbers—he owns bitcoin, ether, and fiat), despite his great achievement in the past year. He still purchases his groceries at the cheapest supermarket and does not own a car, but he hopes to move to southern Germany with his wife. Although he adores Edinburgh, he expresses his dissatisfaction with "the weather." In Germany, he'll most likely purchase a Tesla.

Meanwhile, he's focusing on a top-secret artificial intelligence project, and conducting research for his most recent partnership – with Ice Cube. "It'll be a very compelling piece with a powerful message, and not a 'celebrity cash-grab," Jones says. He also doesn't think the NFT market is overvalued. Rather, it's the perfect setting for the "future of art and e-commerce.”

# CHAPTER 15:

 

# NFT TIPS

 

 

You can filter the Ranking by 24 hours, 7 days, and 30 days. This is a fantastic method to find out what's hot right now.

The Floor Price is the starting point for determining the price of an NFT in the collection. Many of the Top NFTs are quite expensive, costing between 1 and 8 ETH.

When viewing the collection, a blue check indicates whether OpenSea has confirmed it.

You'll need to link a wallet, such as Coinbase or MetaMask. I've tried both and found them to be pretty comparable.

Keep at least $150 on hand for the Ethereum Gas Fee. DO NOT SHARE THE KEY TO YOUR WALLET WITH

ANYONE! Be wary of anyone that offers to help you in Discord because they could be a scammer.

With NFTs, there's no assurance that you'll make money — you could lose everything!

How to Choose an NFT

I used the OpenSea ranking system when I first started. I felt more at ease purchasing something that had already received a high rating. Parallel Alpha was the source of my first NFTs, and they were ranked in the Top 3 at the time.

New projects are generally less expensive, but they may lack a huge following and cannot be confirmed. Here are some more suggestions to get you started:

Check Twitter

Twitter is a fantastic method to stay up to date on the NFT project. You can check how many followers they have, how many people like their posts, how many people comment on them, and so on. This is a fantastic approach to determine how engaged the NFT collection is.

Join our Discord Server.

You can join a Discord channel for most NFTs. This is an excellent method to learn about the project, its creators, and how to get involved. You can view how many individuals are in the Discord, and how many are online and read through the posts. Is the Discord server up and running?

Website/Project Roadmap

On the project website, most NFT projects offer a roadmap for their future ambitions. You can view their marketing, growth, and new product plans. To entice new participants, several initiatives include prizes in their roadmap. They may also have upcoming online events, new releases, or game development plans. # CHAPTER 18:

 

# BLOCKCHAIN GAMING

 

 

Cryptocurrency is gaining popularity, and it appears as if everyone is putting money into this relatively new market. That is one of the main reasons why blockchain technology has caught the interest of every major industry.

The gaming business appears to be one of the first to adopt this technology, but what does the future hold for blockchain gaming?

There is currently a strong overlap between the blockchain and gaming communities. People that are interested in blockchain are also inclined to love and participate in gaming. The popularity of NFTs in the gaming

industry reflects this, with many rare and valuable NFTs being traded within games.

Blockchain is an important component of future financial innovations. The concept of an incorruptible ledger is gaining popularity outside of bitcoin disputes. Developers that want to incorporate user-optimized games can employ blockchain, which is necessary for hosting a decentralized banking system.

 

 

What Is Blockchain Gaming?

A blockchain is a system that employs cryptography to link together chunks of data sequentially. Each data unit on the blockchain is unique and unchangeable, since a change in one block might affect the entire chain. Blockchain games are distinguished from traditional games by the fact that each digital asset is unique. A non-fungible token (NFT) is a unique data unit held on the blockchain for these digital assets.

 

 

NFTs play a significant role in Blockchain gaming.

Blockchain gaming is the name of the industry. Play to earn is a type of blockchain gaming in which a player earns

their unique NFT(s) or cryptocurrency as a result of merit- based game advancements, which they can later convert to real money.

Because each NFT is unique, blockchain technology has incorporated the concept of digital asset ownership. A work of digital art, such as an image, could be easily replicated and used by thousands of people without acknowledging the original creator. If the artwork is kept on the blockchain as a non-fungible token (NFT), the owner can claim the right to keep or sell it to anyone he wants. It's worth noting that this approach works better for games more than art.

Players have complete control over the digital assets they earn or receive as a result of their involvement in blockchain games. Even if players in traditional games pay real money for their digital assets, they will lose access to them if the server is ever down. Both the game assets and the

money would remain the developer's or publisher's property.

On the other hand, players in blockchain games retain complete control of their digital assets, allowing them to freely exchange them with other players, sell them for real money, and potentially use them across many game universes.

 

 

What Are Blockchain Games? Axie Infinity

Blockchain games are developed with blockchain technology or published on the blockchain, so the game isn’t solely owned by one entity, but the entire cluster of computers playing it. To do so, you’ll need a Smart Contract, which is a type of digital contract that governs actions on the blockchain.

Unsurprisingly, gaming is one of the most profitable and widely used blockchain applications. Despite its link with Bitcoin and Ethereum, blockchain is presently the backbone of the NFT trade. The creation of digital assets and the ease with which they can be collected and traded is one of its most essential applications.

 

 

Important Terms To Know in Blockchain Gaming Play-to-Earn

The play-to-earn paradigm is used in most blockchain games, rewarding players for playing games and participating in in- game economies, which allow users to buy and trade game- related commodities.

Unlike traditional game models like free-to-play with in- game purchases or pay-to-play, Blockchain-based games give players ultimate control over their digital assets.

As a result of the system's virtuous circle, players gain benefits such as skins, gaming items, and weaponry.

 

 

Pay-To-Earn

Under the pay-to-earn approach, which is related to play-to- earn, users must make an initial, up-front purchase of an in- game asset, commonly an NFT.

The item can build value over time, similar to the play-to- learn technique, with the prospect of profits serving as an incentive for stickiness.

 

 

GameFi

GameFi, a concept that stresses the earning potential of gaming, refers to both play-to-earn, and pay-to-earn models.

A strong blockchain foundation with fast transaction rates and low fees is required for a successful blockchain game. Consumers will not (and should not) pay exorbitant transaction charges when purchasing or selling items, because they are unusable without them.

 

 

In-Game Asset Ownership

Gamers are hesitant to pay for in-game assets that are not portable and are controlled by the developer. Worse, if a gaming site goes down, gamers lose everything they've invested.

Tokenizing in-game assets, which entails creating NFTs, allows creators to own their assets fully.

 

 

The Role Of NFTs

Reuters estimates that the global NFT market will be worth

$2.5 billion in the first half of 2021, up from $13.7 million in the first two quarters of 2020. Now that NFTs have entered the market, more organizations are keen to build their blockchain gaming businesses.

 

 

 

 

 

NFTs' price

If current trends continue, blockchain-based gaming might become a compelling cryptocurrency use case that disrupts the vast global gaming industry.

 

 

According to a popular website, in August 2020, over 883,000 daily unique wallets interacted with game-related smart contracts, up from 804,000 in July. Since January of this year, these estimates have climbed by over 100%, indicating a more substantial trend gaining traction as more game makers disclose blockchain initiatives.

Overall, blockchain gaming holds great potential: by 2026, the global gaming industry is expected to grow to 314.40 billion USD, up from 173.70 billion USD in 2020. According to current estimates, there are approx. 1 billion online gamers around the world, or approximately one out of every seven individuals.

In-game assets such as weaponry, skins, and experience points have already been tracked using blockchain. These assets cannot be hacked or altered in this manner. Most of these assets are valuable in the game, but they could one day be purchased and exchanged on a third-party marketplace like an NFT.

Due to large money and a growing incentive based on public demand, blockchain appears likely to become a common component among online gaming promotions from various sites in the future.

Blockchain would benefit casinos in addition to boosting interest from players. Gaming companies are required to provide thorough reports with their different regulatory and financial authorities. This operation would be made easier by an incorruptible ledger, providing greater security for crucial information held by individuals and businesses.

 

 

Top Blockchain Games to Keep an Eye On Gala

Gala Furthermore, its price has risen from $0.02 to $0.12, an all-time high, in just a week. However, CoinMarketCap reports that it is presently trading at $0.080.

On the other hand, Gala Games is only a portion of the process of generating a multiverse. For the time being, the project serves as a publisher, a gaming store, and an NFT marketplace. Gala Games is just a first step toward constructing a multiverse. Currently, the project functions as a publisher, a gaming store, and an NFT marketplace. On the other hand, the creators have made no mention of their desire to create a multiverse. It is a realistic choice.

The initiative bills itself as a decentralized network that places a premium on gaming. The blockchain should remain invisible,"

Reasons why blockchain-based gaming economies are the future

MARKET ANALYSIS

The gaming industry has been on fire recently, as everyone who hasn't been living under a rock knows. It's one of the industries that has benefited greatly from the COVID-19 pandemic.

The global gaming industry is now worth $180 billion, making it the fastest-growing form of entertainment on the planet. In terms of yearly revenues, the worldwide film business is valued over $100 billion, whereas all North American sports combined are around $73 billion.

In 2018, three of the top four most-watched sporting events in the US were not conventional sports. They were esports tournaments. For instance, the League of Legends championship drew 30 million more viewers than the AFC Championship, and 45 million more than the NCAA Football Championship.

Travis Scott held a live concert on the famous online game Fortnite last April. It had over 12.3 million views, and made Scott almost $20 million, according to TechCrunch and GamesIndustry.biz.

So, what precisely is going on here, and where does all of this growth originate?

A large part of this may be ascribed to technical improvements and exponential growth. The way we interact, cooperate, generate and consume information, transfer value, and establish online communities is all changing due to technological advancements.

Howard Shultz, the former CEO of Starbucks, popularized the concept of a "third physical space" with his coffee shop

concept. He thought humans needed a "third space" to assemble outside of the office and at home. Starbucks was the solution.

This same notion is currently being played out among today's youth; however, the new commonplace is digital and is referred to as the Metaverse. These are the places where youngsters nowadays are increasingly congregating. They go there to chat with friends, listen to music, or play video games. This can be seen as the next generation of online communities, following Facebook, AOL chat rooms, Myspace, and the Metaverse.

 

 

The Origins of Gaming

In the late 1950s, the first video game, a basic tennis game related to Pong, was invented. The iconic Atari 2600 home video game console made its debut in 1977. In the early 1980s, Nintendo began developing popular arcade games like Donkey Kong, Mario Bros., and others, with subsequent games like The Legend of Zelda being published exclusively on Nintendo's home platform, the Nintendo Entertainment System.

It's important to note that the business model has changed significantly over time. We used to buy a $60 game at a retailer like GameStop. It was a one-time charge that gave you unlimited access to the game. Games were promoted and published in the same way as Hollywood movies are. 90% of the revenue would be generated in the first two weeks.

The Freemium model is the industry standard now. This is basically a business model in which basic services are provided free of charge, while more advanced features must be paid for. Users are enticed to make in-game purchases.

This can now be seen in games such as Roblox, Fortnite, and others.

This is a far more effective technique for game producers, since it keeps their customers interested and continuously updating to compete with their friends. We're coming closer to a world where younger generations use their in-game avatar, weapon, and skins to communicate with one another in the Metaverse. Welcome to the future.

 

Why gaming will move to blockchains

The majority of today's gaming occurs through private data networks. As a result, users will no longer own their in- game assets (avatars, skins, abilities, etc.). They are the platform's property. This approach is being upended by Axie Infinity, which allows users to hold assets on Axie, as nonfungible tokens (NFTs), and trade them for profit in a free market gaming economy.

Annualized income per Token Terminal for an open and permission-less pay-to-play blockchain game is $2.7 billion.

Note: The use of blockchain technology allows players to claim ownership of their in-game assets. This is not possible with today's technology.

Blockchains allow gaming economies to evolve organically. Users can be paid for their participation. Axie Infinity is once again leading the assault. Axie users must first purchase Axie NFTs and the AXS native token before they can begin playing. Individuals may then play and compete for the Smooth Love Potion (SLP) token, which can be exchanged for fiat money or other crypto assets. Many users in the Philippines are earning many times their monthly pay simply by playing Axie Infinity, which is incredible given the economic hardships brought on by COVID-19. Let me pose a question to you: Would you rather be compensated for playing a blockchain game, or not be compensated for playing a non-blockchain game? Charlie Munger says, "Show me the incentives, and I'll show you the result."

Public blockchains have no permissions and are accessible to anybody. Do you have a cell phone and internet access?

You're welcome to participate. Not only can you participate in a blockchain, but also earn money from it. We should anticipate more individuals gaining access to crypto and blockchain-based games shortly, as smartphone penetration increases with the expansion of 4G and 5G technologies in emerging regions.

Open protocols collapse and compress the costs of existing technology. The term "public blockchain" refers to a protocol that is open to the whole public. Ethereum is a protocol that may be used at no cost. Anybody may create games on Etherum. By doing so, one is effectively outsourcing a substantial amount of their operations and capital costs to the Ethereum foundation layer blockchain, making game development easier for entrepreneurs. Low entry barriers aid competition. As a result of this, the end-users benefit. Blockchains are just the next phase in open source technology's progression.

Decentralization

Anybody can build on blockchains because they are permission-less and open. This suggests that in the future, blockchain games built on top of layer-one blockchains like Solana, Ethereum, Cosmos, and others should be expected. Users will be able to swiftly and simply switch games, carrying their assets with them, such as NFTs in the form of weapons, avatars, and skins. This is something that can't be done right now. Users will also be able to make money by selling their NFT assets, or perhaps they will wish to build NFTs? You don't need to possess a gaming platform to take part.

Gaming economies will be the way of the future, and they will be based on blockchain technology.

# CHAPTER 19:

 

# HOW TO BECOME A CRYPTO-ARTIST IN TEN STEPS

 

 

 

It's simple enough for newbies to figure out what an NFT is, but how can I become a CryptoArtist? Is one of the most often asked questions with ambiguous answers. To put it another way, where do I begin, how do I sell and advertise my work, and how do I connect with the CryptoArt community (and, maybe more significantly, where does the CryptoArt community congregate)? We'll go through the fundamentals of how to become a CryptoArtist in this section.

 

 

1.  The Technical Part

Of course, the first step is to open a crypto wallet and convert fiat cash (such as USD or GBP) into Ethereum. To mint your artworks on NFT stores, you'll need ETH.

 

 

2. Know the Different NFT Stores

A general NFT shop, where anybody may sell their artwork, and a curated store, where artists must first be accepted, are the two sorts of NFT stores. OpenSea and Rarible are the two most popular general NFT retailers; however, there are Cargo and Mintable. Platforms such as SuperRare, MakersPlace, KnownOrigin, Nifty Gateway, and BlockParty first vet the artists and require an application. These might

be tough to join for novices with minimal prior social media experience.

 

 

3.           Join the 'CryptoTwitter' Community to Connect with Your Tribe

This is the most effective approach to begin your career as a CryptoArtist. Connecting with other collectors and artists in the NFT environment is an important component of marketing and selling your art. Most of the activity takes place on Twitter, Discord groups, and a few small Facebook communities. It's easy to get started with Twitter and interact with people, and you can use Twitter to identify Discord groups to join. ClubHouse has recently been a favorite hangout location for NFT members.

Look through the artists on marketplaces like KnownOrigin or MakersPlace, and follow the ones that resonate with you on Twitter; from there, you may easily dive into the rabbit hole through other people's retweets and tags. Also, don't forget to chat with the people! Build genuine relationships with the artists, fans, and collectors. Leave comments on the artists' work that you love, express your thoughts, and solicit feedback.

 

 

4.  Know Your Style

As an artist, it's critical to be aware of your distinctive style to stand out. Only a few artists can be differentiated from the rest simply by looking at their work. Understand your brand, message, and personal style.

 

 

5. Create Value for Collectors

Currently, the CryptoArt field is being inundated by short- term, quick-flip ventures with little future value. There is no simple answer to what collectors and investors want, but it is reasonable to assume that they want to buy from artists who can advertise themselves and expand in the field, as well as those who will be present for the long haul. It's usually a good idea to provide value and interest to long- term holders by doing something exceptional, such as special drops. Collectors also like scarcity and exclusivity, knowing that they own a unique edition or a limited-edition piece with just 10 copies.

 

 

6. Shill Your Art to the World

Don't be afraid to shill (show off/promote) your work! Just like with traditional art, marketing is a huge element of selling digital art. And it's becoming increasingly vital as the number of artists using the space increases. If you're selling on OpenSea or Rarible, you'll have to rely totally on your marketing skills, as these are generic markets open to anybody; therefore, you'll need to make sure your artworks are noticed.

Check out which social media platforms are best for your job, and don't be afraid to join groups or advertise yourself on Twitter, but don't go overboard. People will find your CryptoArt profile if you are active on social media.

 

 

If you want to add credibility and a "brand image" to your work, you'll need a website and a portfolio. Many CryptoArt sites will need you to submit a portfolio, so having one ready is always a smart idea. Having a website allows potential collectors to learn more about you and your unique narrative. Collectors aren't only interested in the artwork; they're also interested in the narrative and the person behind it.

 

 

One way not to market yourself is by spamming links to your CryptoArt in a direct message. It's lazy, and it'll almost certainly work against you. Engage with your audience if you're going to communicate a message about your art.

Make your message unique. Copying and pasting the same message without personalization, as well as identifying major collectors or companies in your public postings, is not a smart idea.

 

 

7.  Be Persistent

Keep in mind that the CryptoArt sector, as well as NFTs in general, is very new. This is only the beginning, so don't be discouraged if you aren't successful right away, or as rapidly as you anticipated. The group is warm and inclusive, and everyone is willing to help one other thrive. Share your work with the world, make modifications as needed, and don't be hesitant to seek assistance.

 

8. Pricing Your Artworks

Keep in mind that you'll have to pay for gas

and minting when pricing your artworks (unless you are using Cargo's Magic Minting option or OpenSea's gasless minting option). If you include the cost of minting and gas, each piece will cost between $50 and $80. To generate a reasonable profit, your painting would have to be substantially more expensive.

 

 

Similarly, if you have done little to nothing to establish your brand, you could get lucky and make a few sales, but your career will most likely be short-lived. Appropriately price your artwork. Just because someone sold their CryptoArt for 1 ETH does not mean you will too. You must establish your brand, which takes time and effort.

 

9.  Start with a Collection

It's a good idea to start by selling a collection of three to eight pieces of artwork. There should be a story and a theme to the collection. This encourages collectors to purchase many pieces from you since having the entire series is always desirable.

 

 

10.  Exhibit in the Metaverses

After you've gained a better understanding of the CryptoArt scene, you can start exploring the metaverses; Decentraland, CryptoVoxels, and Somnium Space. Each week, art displays are held in virtual galleries in these metaverses. This is a fantastic method to promote your work, meet new people, and even do interviews. You can interact with the individuals who run the galleries and put up an exhibition by following their Twitter accounts. They often retweet about the newest builds and exhibits going, so you can connect with them and set up an exhibition.

Best of luck to all of the artists on their path to becoming CryptoArtists. There is a lot of promise in the field, and there are a lot of fresh and amazing initiatives coming out every day. Remember to connect, express your individuality, and keep creating!

 

# CONCLUSION

 

 

The popularity of non-fungible tokens is rapidly growing. It's a new way for people to sell their digital assets online while maintaining their authenticity. Although it's a brand- new front in the blockchain ecosystem, we can believe it's here to stay based on its huge success. Many companies are now implementing blockchain technologies using this new type of token.

NFTs are great for creating memorable experiences for your customers. They're also a fantastic way to connect with, and engage your target audience.

Brands must pay careful attention to the technology as it is still in its infancy. More precisely, you need to research ways you can use NFTs in your marketing campaigns. For instance, you can mint luxury product designs, produce memorable marketing campaigns, or partner with NFT creators.

In the end, NFT technology is here to stay, and it will certainly play a role in digital marketing.
